#14537 1 2007-03-23T02:24:57Z Gforce20
NOOB MAPS!!!!!!11!11one

As the title suggests, this contest deals with N00B maps. Here is your mission: Scour NUMA to find the N00Biest maps you can get your hands on, then change it so that it's at least a decent map. Here are a few quick rules:

1. The map must not have a rating higher than 2 (if it's not rated, you'll have to use your own judgement skills).
2. Your revised map must not change the map's type (for example, don't turn a N00B Survival map into a decent Race map).
3. Your map must resemble the N00B map in some manner (if you give both pictures to somebody, they should be able to tell that they're similar).
4. Revising a DDA is okay, but other non-playable maps (Incomplete, N Art, Tilesets) are not allowed.
5. All submissions need to be submitted as an external file or in Numa, NOT IN YOUR POST!
6. All maps NEED an original. And it can't be yours.

I'll need some judges... if anybody's willing to judge. I'll take as many as I can get.
Unlimited entrants, unless there are extenuating circumstances (such as too much bandwidth being used... not that that will happen... )

No concrete deadline picked yet. I'll decide on that when there are a good number of people willing to play.

Submissions:
Remix of Quicky Mart by Bunniesandsheep (Original)
Black. Noir. Negro. (Remix of the drones) by max_ride (Original)
(Unnamed) by 29403 (Original link, please?)

Judges:
29403 (unconfirmed)

#14670 1 2007-03-31T22:00:23Z Gforce20
This is something me and my friend Delgadude studied for awhile. Through strange glitches and manual code editing, you can create all the effects seen on the demo in the attached file.
In the same order as the demo, here are the glitches:

1. Offset placement of one-way (horizontal)
Created by: Manual code editing
How-to: First, you should know that it works best to use this glitch with a whole row of one-ways. So first, you should make your row of one-ways, while placing the wall that you want to be glitched slightly off. This is what you should have:

Next, exit the object editor by pressing Home. (Before you continue, make sure that your row of walls was placed last and your offset wall was placed last of all.) Go to the bottom of the code and look for repeating numbers:
Quote
7^156,324,3!7^180,324,3!7^204,324,3!7^252,324,3!7^276,324,3!7^300,324,3!7^228,318,3
Highlighted in red is your offset variable. Change this number to something even closer to the repeating digits, such as 324.01 or 323.99 (I'd suggest putting it within .01 in order to make it appear connected). Now your walls should look like they're going in a straight line. However, when N walks over the edited wall, he's sure to fall unless he decides to jump!

2. Wall-jump through the ceiling
Created by: In-game glitch
How-to: All you really need to do in this glitch is put one-way walls facing down with a wall you can wall-jump from next to it. Example:

To activate this glitch, jump into the corner where the one-way ceiling and your wall-jump surface meet. You must be as close to this corner as possible. Simply wall-jump next to get through.

3. Offset placement of one-way (vertical)
Created by: Manual code editing
How-to: Creating this glitch is done the same way as glitch 1. Activating it is different, however: First, your glitched one-way must not be at the very top (it must have a wall you can wall-slide on above it). In order to go through it, get as close as possible to the wall above it. Wall-sliding is the most reliable way to do this. Make sure you're pushing the arrow key against the wall as you slide. When you hit the glitched wall, you'll slip right through!

4. Gold delay through the wall
Created by: Gold delay
How-to: Just place a gold piece 1/4 below the one-way wall. Example:
-Activated simply by walking or wall-sliding on the one-way.

5. Walk through one-way on a slope
Created by: Non-natural angle
How-to: Make a slope that touches the one-way wall. Example:
-Activated by walking under the ceiling while on the slope.

6. Thin Invisible Surface
Created by: Excess surface on one-way
How-to: This is a very useful glitch for getting out of a tight squeeze where walljumping isn't available. Make a real wall (not a one-way) that goes up. Put a one-way wall just inside it, so that you can't see any of it, but it's touching the edge where wall and air meet. If you're using other Ned tiles besides E, you might be able to see it. Put one of these one-ways every 1-3 spaces. When using this, hold the arrow key against the wall the entire time; jump to find the next invisible surface. This also works on the floor, except here you'll just be stopped from walking a certain direction (you'll need to jump just a little).
Also, you can connect one-ways with up to 3/4 grid space between each one, and still have a completely flat surface due to this.

7. Slip through one-way via connected slope
Created by: Non-natural angle
Partial credit to exuberance for pointing out the fact that I missed this glitch.
How-to: Place a slope next to a one-way wall floor. Walk very slowly up the slope, and when you reach the one-way where the slope tiles end and there is space behind it, you'll tip off the edge (or if you're using this to go through a wall, you'll just fall through.) This is possibly due to the extra surface past the visible one-way wall.

8. Thwump through a floor/ceiling
Created by: In-game glitch
Partial credit to exuberance (ditto #7)
How-to: Simply make a thwump that goes through your one-way surface upon falling. Get onto the thwump, then stay on it until you pass through the wall. If you're going through a floor rather than a ceiling, just stay below the thwump (which should be facing up). IMPORTANT: If you're going through a ceiling, make sure to JUMP as soon as you're getting squeezed between the thwump and the one-way, or else it might get messy...

9. Usage of door through a surface
Created by: Non-natural angle
Full credit to exuberance for discovering this.
How-to: Use 1/2-snap to place your surface so that it's not on a full snap, but rather halfway. Then put a trapdoor, locked door, or normal door the same way as your one-way. Example:


10. Bounce block through a surface
Created by: In-game glitch
How-to: Place a bounce block one tile away from an opposing one-way surface. Make the bounce block move around so that you can touch the surface of the one-way and have the bounce block hit you. When that happens, you'll slip through.

11. Cliff-hanging through oneway
Created by: Non-natural angle
Note: This glitch is very similar to number 9.
When you place your one-way, make note of its position. If you put a slope or sharp edge less than 1 grid-snap away from it, you can fall through by touching it. Examples:
<Any of the ninjas can get through those one-ways unconventionally.

12. One-way teleportation brake
Created by: Lack of pulverization
Ordinarily a teleporter can kill you by slamming you hard into the solid wall or ceiling, as well as the floor. However, if you put a one-way far away enough from the wall, it will stop the ninja in his tracks without killing him.

13. Specially placed one-ways
Created by: Unknown
The strangest one-way glitch I've ever seen. Place two one-ways: one with a y-coordinate of 264, and the other 132, both facing up. Fall straight off of the higher one onto the lower- you'll fall through with one frame where you're on it.

14. NaN'd position
Created by: NaN
Replace the last number in the code with a non-numeric value (NaN it). It will appear to be facing up, but it really acts as if it's pointing down.

15. Launchpad through one-way
Created by: Invincibility frame
Upon hitting a launchpad while next to a one-way, you will be able to go through the one-way. This is because one-ways are infinitely thin; the slightest propulsion beyond them will go unnoticed if you're invincible (as you are when you hit a launchpad).
Note: This glitch isn't yet in the demo.

#311075 2 2007-04-07T23:31:20Z Gforce20
Quote
I don't have a tutorial, but here are some pieces of advice:

1. Don't make too short DDAs. Nobody likes them.
2. Make sure it has many close calls. At least I think they're cool smile.gif
3. Put in some more objects than just launch pads. A good DDS has at least some enemies, tiles, trap doors, normal doors, gold. AND the some oh-so-famous launch pads.
4. An impressive DDS also uses some game glitches and bugs, for example - you can use gold delay and/or thwump launches.
5. As much as people hate too simple DDAs, it shouldn't have too many objects. If it has, prepare to see some serious lag.
Take a good look at #s 2, 3 and 5.
#311082 501 2007-04-07T23:52:05Z Gforce20

Checking to see if posting your level data will corrupt it. Results: The only way you can corrupt your level data by posting it is word wrap.

ED: should have 2 letters
01

1: shoud have 4 letters
3245

2: shoud have 4 letters
GFHI

3: shoud have 4 letters
?>@A

4: shoud have 4 letters
7689

5: shoud have 4 letters
QPNO

6: shoud have 4 letters
KJLM

7: shoud have 4 letters
CBDE

8: shoud have 4 letters
;:<=

Demo data: has numbers 0-9, the | symbol, and a colon : in it. Should have 11 letters
123456789:|

Now in code format. People often use this.

ED: should have 2 letters
01

shoud have 4 letters
3245

2: shoud have 4 letters
GFHI

3: shoud have 4 letters
?>@A

4: shoud have 4 letters
7689

5: shoud have 4 letters
QPNO

6: shoud have 4 letters
KJLM

7: shoud have 4 letters
CBDE

8: shoud have 4 letters
;:<=

Demo data: has numbers 0-9, the | symbol, and a colon : in it. Should have 11 letters
123456789:|

Quote

I'm not guessing at all here. "And" is a conjunction, used for connecting two independent clauses, two subordinate clauses, two phrases, or two parts of speech (two nouns, two verbs, etc.). You can't use it to start a sentence. Also, an ellipsis is three dots spaced out like so: . . . and is used when you delete words in the middle of a quotation. It, too, can't be used to start a sentence.

Admit it, man. You just got owned.

#318747 21 2007-05-06T04:44:57Z Gforce20
Hey, you can do a REALLY cool thing in sandbox mode. It looks like white noise with gray and black shades, and it forms into strange shapes. Here's how to do it:

1. Enter Sandbox mode and press Clear.
2. Set the shading to Plasma Shader.
3. No lines.
4. Use the Blackbody color-set-thingy.
5. Turn on 3-D lighting.
6. Switch cursor to Particle Mode.
7. Switch Viscosity and Jello on.
8. Set the color to black dye.
9. Click the left button on the mouse and hold. Move it around just a little while you hold.
10. Switch cursor to Sink Mode.
11. Quickly click and let go, then let the show begin!

#324228 55 2007-05-25T22:07:39Z Gforce20
Dude, here is like the best use for this ever.

Enter AVS editor, make new visualization, add effect Trans / Color Clip. Set the outcolor to 121 121 136 (walls). Make sure that you're also clearing each frame (Main). Then set the transparent color (Settings > Display > Overlay mode) to the object color you want; the objects of that color all have the wall color.

Set the overlay mode to 202 202 208 (background color in N) for an all-walls surprise. You can see the borders of tiles other than E, D, and 5, but everything else is all walls. Set it to bounceblock color and you have some tiles that bounce.

#329403 181 2007-06-10T01:25:40Z Gforce20
Quote: sweep, December 17, 2006 04:23 pm
Here are some of the essential problems with the system:
  • The focus of the website is placed too strongly on ratings. If you want to browse maps and want to avoid the poor stuff, you'll head straight for the top rated charts. We want browsing NUMA to be different - you should be able to find maps by difficulty, tags, favorites and bitesizing. Being able to search by ratings is brilliant, but it sends out the wrong message - it makes people think that ratings are the be all and end all. Well, they shouldn't be the be all. Or the end all.
  • People aren't scared of being caught. Whilst Arachnid was in charge, he wasn't actively searching out multi-accounters. That's fair enough, he's a busy man, but it meant that anyone who wasn't a bit low in the brain cell department could easily cheat the system to their benefit. People need to know that they will be caught. Systems need to be in place to detect vote fraud.
  • People who commit vote fraud and get away with it find swaying the average rating of maps very easy to do. A single 0 on a map with four existing ratings of 5 has the effect of dropping the average rating by 4. There are ways round this: namely histograms and mechanisms to discard outliers automatically.
NUMA needs work. It needs restructuring. This is all very possible. We have ideas, we have plans, maestro has smarts and I have more solutions than we could ever need.

But something bugs me particularly. Discovering that so many people have been multi-accounting (pending even more likely additions to the list in the near future) has spelt it out loud and clear: all voting that has been made since the beginning of NUMA is corrupted. Every single map that has seen the top rated page very probably has some kind of suspicious activity in its records. I hate this. I hate the idea that there are traces of these crimes throughout every nook and cranny of NUMA, and I am keen to start over.

What I want to suggest is by no means certain. It's just something I wanted to throw out there, and something that maestro is keen to hear the community's response to. I know a lot of people may react badly to it. I am open to hearing what you guys think. My suggestion is drastic, and at the moment just a potential road that we've pondered.

I want to wipe the slate clean. I want to reset all the voting on NUMA, removing every vote from the system. No exceptions. See it as a detox. Washing all the crap out of the system. Starting again.

If we were to do this, we wouldn't completely discard the old ratings - we would save all the old average ratings to the database, so people could browse via that and all the voting of the past isn't completely wasted. Equally, the resetting of the votes would be done alongside introducing measures to solve the flaws in the system mentioned above. We would change the focus of the website, and start over again.

If you hate the idea, we want to hear it. We want to hear what you think we should do instead. If you love the idea, we want to hear your response too.

So, when will all this take place? I really hope it happens soon because now twice I've felt the sting of snipers.

#330434 60 2007-06-12T16:54:18Z Gforce20
Quote: Exberance, June 11, 2007 01:23 am
Quote: Gforce20, May 25, 2007 05:07 pm
Dude, here is like the best use for this ever.

Enter AVS editor, make new visualization, add effect Trans / Color Clip. Set the outcolor to 121 121 136 (walls). Make sure that you're also clearing each frame (Main). Then set the transparent color (Settings > Display > Overlay mode) to the object color you want; the objects of that color all have the wall color.

Set the overlay mode to 202 202 208 (background color in N) for an all-walls surprise. You can see the borders of tiles other than E, D, and 5, but everything else is all walls. Set it to bounceblock color and you have some tiles that bounce.
Next time the moving tiles suggestion comes up....

BAM! Redirect [here]

That would work!

Also, set Trans / Color Clip's outcolor to 202 202 208 and make the overlay color the color of zap drones. Now you have blind, fast laser drones... some that are heat-seeking!

#15736 1 2007-06-17T04:43:54Z Gforce20
Contest closed
SURVIVOR N Island


INSTRUCTIONS:
Congratulations, all participants will be instantly swept away to an island full of survival challenges! These maps may vary from custom-built maps to previously submitted ones by other authors. As in the real show Survivor, there will be outcasts, special challenges, the occasional puzzle, and of course people getting voted off. However, while the strongest mambers of a team may pose a threat to the others winning, they're also crucial to getting higher scores on the team challenges, which can greatly help your score. Meanwhile, the lower-ranked members of your team may be a weak link, but pose no threat to the average player. This challenge is about both skill-testing and social rankings.
THE COURSE:
Depending on the number of players, there will be a varied number of challenges throughout the competition, but there will be about 1/3 Survivals, 1/4 Puzzles, 1/5 Races, and the remainder a mixture of these. The challenges will be scored upon multiple ideas, often adding all the members' scores together or averaging them. Gold may or may not affect your team's score, depending on the type of challenge. After two challenges have been played out, each member will send me a Private Message saying who they vote to be cast off the island. The member with the most votes will be disqualified.
You will have 5 3 days to post your demo. In the event of a tie, you will have 2 days to decide who will play the tiebreaking third map and 2 days for the two players to submit their demos.
BONUSES:
There will be a number of surprises throughout the challenge. For example, players who get higher scores on some races and survivals may receive the Immunity Idol. For those of you who don't know, that means that that person cannot be voted off for the next voting. Also, the team members may also have to send one to Exile Island, where the two members (one from each team) will be sent away for the next challenge.
NOTE:
There will be no judges, as this competition is not based upon opinion, but purely skill and time.
TEAMS:

TEAM RED

mam (dropped out)

TEAM BLUE

Ghost player
Spoiler: Challenge 1: (click to view)
Each team member will have 5 days to get your record time on this Survival map. Your scores will all be averaged based upon frames.

How to send me your demos: Post it here as a .txt attached file. Whatever you submit becomes final, so keep improving your run until you can't do any better!
Spoiler: Challenge 2:Challenge 2:

Each team member will have 5 days to get your record time on this Puzzle map. Your scores will be added together. Gold collection adds 10 frames per gold.

Post demos in the same way as the previous challenge. If you are not able to complete it, submit a demo to as far as you can get; scoring will be sub-based upon how many drones you passed in that case.

#15750 1 2007-06-17T22:20:53Z Gforce20
This program generates the object code for a spirograph in N. It requires the input of at least 5 values, one of which determines the object. Some examples of spirographs in N are here.

I don't have the proper licsenses to make a real EXE from Liberty BASIC, but my friend does. I e-mailed him about it and he may or may not give me an actual program of it. Anyway, here's the source code:

xc=300
yc=300
steps=0.01
10 PROMPT "Enter radius of fixed circle (1-99)"; R
20 PROMPT "Enter radius of moving circle (1-99)"; r
30 PROMPT "Enter offset point (1-99)"; O
31 PROMPT "Enter length (1-20)"; endpoint
32 PROMPT "Enter object code number (See the Readme file)"; object
40 CONFIRM "Debug?"+chr$(13)+"Click yes if your previous spirograph"+chr$(13)+"was cut off the screen.";var$
50 IF var$="yes" THEN 150
55 PRINT "Working... (program copyright Gforce20 xanaman@gmail.com)"
60 FOR t=1 TO endpoint STEP steps
70 x =(R+r)*cos(t)-(r+O)*cos(((R+r)/r)*t)
80 y =(R+r)*sin(t)-(r+O)*sin(((R+r)/r)*t)
90 lvl$=lvl$+str$(object)+"^"+str$(x+xc)+","+str$(y+yc)+"!"
100 NEXT
110 length=len(lvl$)-1
120 level$=left$(lvl$,length)
125 CLS
130 PRINT level$
140 END
150 PROMPT "Enter center point X-coordinate (default 300)"; xc
160 PROMPT "Enter center point Y-coordinate (default 300)"; yc
170 PROMPT "Enter step value"+chr$(13)+"higher value is lower quality (default 0.01)"; steps
175 IF steps=0 THEN NOTICE "Cannot step 0; infinite loop" : GOTO 170
180 CONFIRM "Change all values to integers? (default No)"; int$
190 IF int$="yes" THEN 215
200 GOTO 60
210 IF var$="yes" THEN 150
215 PRINT "Working in debug mode... (program copyright Gforce20 xanaman@gmail.com)"
220 FOR t=1 TO endpoint STEP steps
230 x =(R+r)*cos(t)-(r+O)*cos(((R+r)/r)*t)
240 y =(R+r)*sin(t)-(r+O)*sin(((R+r)/r)*t)
250 lvl$=lvl$+"10^"+str$(int(x+xc))+","+str$(int(y+yc))+"!"
260 NEXT
270 length=len(lvl$)-1
280 level$=left$(lvl$,length)
285 CLS
290 PRINT level$
300 END

Download Liberty BASIC from www.libertybasic.com and run the code to use it. Readme is in attached file. READ IT.

The laser searches for a collision (with a tile) on a line between it and the ninja until it finds one, then it draws the laser. Creating a room with all mirrors and a laser drone will only make it so that the laser will never detect a collision, so the laser will never be drawn and the game will crash.

The laser searches for a collision (with a tile, or closed door, or deployed trapdoor, or locked normal door) on a line between it and the ninja until it finds one, then it draws the laser. Assuming that the mirror has the same tile-based properties as the aforementioned three door choices which also have collision properties applied to them, the laser makes a check.

If Collision=True, and CollisionObject=Mirror then reflect the beam to an angle corresponding to the firing angle.

Problem solved.

#16090 1 2007-07-06T20:09:09Z Gforce20
I have it, and I like how it looks, but there are plenty of cons:

-Lags N
-Hogs RAM
-UAC is annoying
-Doesn't support old help files
-Too user-friendly; not very customizable
-Larger system requirements

The pros:

-New style
-More driver support
-Faster loadup
-Overall better performance
-Improved Windows Genuine Advantage

Do you think it's really worth the extra money?

After reading this topic, I lol'd and barfed at the same time.
I want you all to picture this. Actually picture this happening.

Gforce comes inside the house after a long day of playing football in the park, as kids do these days. (He made two goals, but his team still lost. No matter, though, because mom still took the guys for pizza afterward.) It was a sunny day but not too hot. Not uncomfortably so. Satisfied with his day, Gforce decides to go on the family computer and check the N forums. He drops off his gear in the living room (which he knows will get him yelled at later -- it always does) and heads into the den. The walls are adorned with books and model cars, and a few stray bowling trophies. This room is mostly where the finances are handled, but it's also home to the computer. A Godsend of the modern world. Gforce slowly presses the power button, not knowing the horrors about to befall him, and the fans all spin an ominous howl.

News forum: SUBLiME winners announced. That's cool, but I saw this a few days ago.
NUMA Discussion: Someone got sniped, someone got bitesized, nobody agrees with what everyone else is rating.
Rate A Signature Game: Haha, this one has a Kablizzy quote. That Kablizzy. Eight out of ten.
Bash N' Thrash... Oh, what's this? LittleViking001 thread? I wonder what they have to say about him.

And so Gforce opens the thread. "With his eyes open," "With his eyes closed," and what on Earth is The Jackhammer? "SlappyMcGee and I couldn't decide on whether LV would keep his eyes open or closed during love-making..." blue_tetris and Kablizzy sure know a lot about this topic. Consciously, he wonders why, but deep down, he already knows. Sweat beads up on Gforce's forehead as he continues to read. The perpetual wink? The helmet tease? The fancy restaurant special? A cringe builds up in his stomach. Is it a cringe of laughter or a cringe of disgust? Sometimes you don't have to choose.

A snicker develops into a chuckle and into a laugh, and soon it becomes uncontrollable. The cringe from before becomes a gag trigger, and before he can stop it, surges of half-digested food make their way up his unwelcoming throat. The congratulatory pizza teams up with Gforce's underhanded stomach acids to score a goal against his tonsils. Yet, all the while, he can't stop laughing. Laughing, laughing, laughing, as a geyser of vomit is expelled from his mouth. A mannequin grin is plastered on his face. Helplessly confused, Gforce tries to close the thread and make it go away. But it doesn't help. It's said on the internet that things you see can't be un-seen.

Chunks of food slide down his dripping chin when he stops to catch his breath, then once again without hesitation, the chortle begins again.


(The moral of the story is that no, you did not lol and barf at the same time.)

#341433 2 2007-07-10T18:28:52Z Gforce20
I had trouble with this level too, but once you have a strategy it's easy. Here's how I do it:

Build up momentum jumping up each wall. Jump from crevice to crevice, allowing the rockets to hit the wall each time before continuing. If you have enough momentum, you can skip a crevice. Once you near the edge, the rocket to that side may see you as you're jumping. That's where the rocket-dodging skill comes in- stay close to the edge as it nears you, then jump away from the wall momentarily and the rocket will get confused and likely hit the wall when it tries to come back.

#344270 5 2007-07-17T23:40:09Z Gforce20
Quote: Rock_man ZX, July 17, 2007 11:08 pm
i couldn't find the numa discussion and the way to do that to your maps is probably confusing like the rest of numa
Not exactly. There are just a few things to know about sorting maps:

It all starts with http://numa.notdot.net/browse? -with the question mark.

After that, add any of these:

sort=[rating,created,votes,random] : Sorts the maps according to any of those. Default created.
order=[asc,desc] : Displays the sorted maps from first to last, or last to first. Default desc.
count=# : Change # to the number of maps displayed per page. Default 10.
start=# : Skips # maps from the start. Default 0, adds (count #) per each page you view.
q=value1+value2+...+valuex : Only displays maps with the specified tags. Common tags are "rated", "unrated", "bitesized", "author:AuthorName", and "playable" or "nonplayable". For "author:authorName", replace authorName with the author's name (i.e. "author:gforce20").

Separate those with the ampersand (&).
Good example:

http://numa.notdot.net/browse?sort=votes&order=asc&count=5&start=0&q=rated+author:ska

This sorts according to votes in ascending order, showing 5 per page, skipping no maps from the start, has a rating, and the author is Ska.

#346692 4 2007-07-23T22:37:50Z Gforce20
I'm still waiting for my friend, he might be busy right now. But I have some good news:

In v1.3, expect an even better compression! Here's what else might happen to the code before being completely zipped:

At the beginning of each object code, you see #^ and # is a number from 0-12. These will all be replaced with a unique character, so that !number^ will be replaced with a character that doesn't recur anywhere else in the code, except for other !number^ things. This changes as many as 4 characters into one.

All numeric values will be replaced with base-64 before compression.

Something might happen with the tileset code, but I can't promise anything amazing. Maybe just removing the | that separates objects from tile code.

v1.4 will probably be the last.

#347275 9 2007-07-25T15:57:10Z Gforce20
Quote: cman4, July 24, 2007 07:47 pm
But wouldn't you have to decrypt it in order to play it? o_o

Yes. The purpose of this program is to help people with not enough hard disk space save a few extra levels by compressing them. v1.2 can compress multiple levels, but with the release of v1.3, the data will be restricted to one level each.

And @LV, what do you mean? Do the 0's represent binary? And there are 34 total characters, which would use 8-bit to 6-bit compression (ASCII to base64), but it would also screw up the latter process which involves removing every 8th bit. That's why it wouldn't work.
If there were two fewer characters involved in tileset codes, I could convert 8-bit to 5-bit with no trouble, until the binary removal process came in... It's just too difficult to do and it would have miniscule compression. Maybe 5%, just for the tileset. It's probably not worth it.

I remember knowing how to do that desktop change thing (like with the start button/menu, taskbar, etc.), but I forget now. Could somebody PM me with an answer?
Yeah, it's not very intuitive.
Usually in Windows you have to right-click on stuff you want to change, but when you want to change the wallpaper, for whatever reason those drunken programmers thought of (maybe it was intentionally counter-intuitive), you have to right-click on the wallpaper, of all places. I know, it beats the hell out of me, too.
If you want to change the properties of something, you usually ignore the 'Properties' entry in the menu. I mean, that's what would make sense, but apparently the fiends at Microsoft wanted to make the interface as bewildering as possible. I was clicking on the obvious choice, the sensible choice, that was "Paste Shortcut" over and over again but was still unable to change the properties of the desktop. I felt hopeless until I thought, "what would be the least sensible thing to do if I wanted to change the properties of the desktop?" Then it hit me - those bastards probably hid it in a choice completely unrelated to the desktop's properties: the 'Properties' entry on the menu you get when you right-click the desktop!
I seriously don't get how Microsoft expects us to find these things.
But then this hideously complicated box comes up with literally thousands of buttons and tabs and spinning wheels... well, it was more tabs than I could count on my nose, anyway (I have trouble when I count past 'one'). So the appearance of the desktop was probably, I figured, on the tab next to the one that said 'Desktop': 'Screen Saver'. After mucking around with that over the course of the week, I accidentally hit the 'Desktop' tab and got where I wanted to go. I should've known to look in the spot it was least likely to be!
But then it showed me a huge selection of wallpapers that I didn't want to use, and it wasn't letting me look for my own. If only there was some way to BROWSE through my hard drive instead of using the list to the left of the button that said BR-

I'm done. Hopefully you get the point.

#348465 26 2007-07-29T01:57:11Z Gforce20
Quote: LittleViking001, July 28, 2007 03:34 pm
Quote: Gforce20, July 28, 2007 01:51 pm
What were you using before it changed to RGB? I don't know of any other color format.
If the image is in Indexed Color, it has to be converted to RGB before a lot of editing options become available. Indexed means that only certain colors are allowed. For example, an 8 color image is indexed, 32 color is indexed, 8-bit (256 color) can be indexed.

Oh... something similar happens in .GIFs, but they only support 256 colors max.

#16972 1 2007-08-26T23:39:38Z Gforce20
Map Combination Contest

The Map Combination Contest is a different kind of tournament: every week, the contestants will make a map based only upon certain elements. They choose their own elements for each week, but no element may be used twice. This leads to many different combinations of styles.

There must be exactly 7 or 8 contestants for this to work.

Since there are three categories of elements and 8 elements in each of those categories, the maps will be quite hard to make well. This is intentional- it shows just how well you can make maps with such restricted themes.

The categories are Tiles, Genres, and Enemies. Since some tiles work together, you may use each group twice. There are only 5 major genres, so Action and Puzzle are repeated, and you may choose the 8th. There are 8 enemies, so that works out quite nicely.

There will be only seven rounds because the 8th would only have one contestant. Hence in each element category there will be one unused element.

Quote For Emphasis: You choose your OWN 3 ELEMENTS each week. I do not dictate them.

Judging:

Tile usage w/10
Relevance to genre x/10
Enemy usage y/10
Fun to play z/10

Total: ?/50

The attached text file shows you the elements. Contestants please keep a copy of it so you can track what you've used.
Attachment (lost): post-21-1188171578.txt

#361774 5 2007-09-03T04:05:32Z Gforce20
The ability to enter or exit dimensions as I choose.

Why?

Because the universe we live in has 3.5 dimensions: 3 matter dimensions and half a time dimension (we can only go forward). When you think about it, if there were a 2d person, he/she could only see in those two dimensions. If you're in three dimensions, you wouldn't have to be in the same plane as the 2d person. Hence you could turn totally invisible and re-enter the world at any place. You could also go backwards in time, or even look down at time and choose where to go based upon what you see from a 2nd time dimension.

#387044 52 2007-12-07T03:28:37Z Gforce20
Quote: Tsukatu, September 03, 2007 05:20 am
Quote: Gforce20, September 02, 2007 11:05 pm
The ability to enter or exit dimensions as I choose.

Why?

Because the universe we live in has 3.5 dimensions: 3 matter dimensions and half a time dimension (we can only go forward). When you think about it, if there were a 2d person, he/she could only see in those two dimensions. If you're in three dimensions, you wouldn't have to be in the same plane as the 2d person. Hence you could turn totally invisible and re-enter the world at any place. You could also go backwards in time, or even look down at time and choose where to go based upon what you see from a 2nd time dimension.
I'd be scared to death to involve a fourth dimension, cuz I could just walk off, or trip, and never be able to find that exact fourth-dimensional depth to be back in the world I know and love.

This is assuming that I know what I'm doing though.

Another power I would like is the ability to psychokinetically shift matter. This is a fairly broad power- it would give me the ability to fly (constantly shifting myself up and forward), move objects with my mind (duh), and allow me to go through solid walls (I'd have to destroy them first though).

#455451 26 2008-07-28T05:38:39Z Gforce20
Okay, I'm going to revive this project with a new plan in mind.
Rather than simply turning it from 8-bit to 7-bit, I'm going to compress the file based upon level data's structure, not just the fact that it's ASCII.

Tile data will be 5-bit indexed, unless 33 or more of the 34 possible tiles are used, in which case it'll have a 1-bit header for 1's and 0's (blank and solid tiles) which will be further compressed, probably based upon repeating patterns.

The object data will work quite differently. Most objects look something like this:
0^32,150!
Each section can be compressed individually. The first section (object type) can be compressed down to 4 bits (I think... I forget how many objects there are O_o), the coordinates can be converted into 10-bit integers, and any extra data like drone paths can be compressed as well using similar methods.

Any questions/suggestions for this new version? It might be coded in PHP, rather than Liberty BASIC, since not many people seem to have Liberty BASIC installed.
